satisfies this condition for
J^{\prime}
, we know that

\mathbf{\bot}(P_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}.

(10)

We want to prove that
\mathbf{\bot}(P_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[u]}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}.

Observe that, for each
S\in\{\mathsf{subtree}(u),\mathsf{subtree}[u]\}
, the set

\mathbf{\bot}(P_{|_{S}})
is a disjoint union of
\mathbf{\bot}^{V}(P_{|_{S}})
and
\mathbf{\bot}^{E}(\mathbf{\hat{g}})
.
From (
10
), we know that
\mathbf{\bot}^{E}(\mathbf{\hat{g}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}
. Thus, it remains to prove that
\mathbf{\bot}^{V}(P_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[u]}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}
.
As
\mathsf{subtree}[u]=\mathsf{subtree}(u)\cup\{u\}
, by
Lemma

5.10
, we have

\mathbf{\bot}^{V}(P_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[u]}})=\mathbf{\bot}^{V}(P_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})\cup\mathbf{\bot}^{V}(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})

(11)

because
\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u}
and
\mathtt{pd}_{u}
are the restrictions of
\mathbf{\hat{f}},\hat{\mathbb{E}}
and
\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}}
on
\{u\}
.
The property
J\in\mathcal{J}
implies the inclusion
\mathbf{\bot}^{V}(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\subseteq\mathtt{F}
.
Moreover, (
10
) implies that
\mathbf{\bot}^{V}(P_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}
holds. Hence, we deduce from (
11
) that
\mathbf{\bot}^{V}(P_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[u]}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}
. Thus,
P
satisfies
(Respect-

F

)
for
I
.

We conclude that
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}(I)
and this proves the present claim.
∎

Claim 6.13
.

For every
J=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}
and every
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}]
, we have
a_{u}(P)=a_{u}[P]+a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}
,
b_{u}(P)=b_{u}[P]
, and
\bar{c}_{u}(P)=\bar{c}_{u}[P]+\bar{c}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}
.

Proof of the claim.

Let
J=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}
and let
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}]
.
We start by proving
a_{u}(P)=a_{u}[P]+a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}
.
By definition, we have

a_{u}(P)=\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}[u]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C).

Recall that
a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}
is the sum of
\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)
over all
C\in
with
\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)<d_{\varphi}
.
Moreover,
a_{u}[P]
is the sum of
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)
over all
v\in\mathsf{subtree}(u)
and
C\in
with
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}
.
As
\mathsf{subtree}[u]=\mathsf{subtree}(u)\cup\{u\}
and
\mathbb{E}_{u}
is the restriction of
\hat{\mathbb{E}}
on
\{u\}
, we have
a_{u}(P)=a_{u}[P]+a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}
.

Now, observe that
b_{u}(P)=b_{u}[P]
because
\mathsf{tss}(u)
and
\mathsf{tss}[u]
have the same broom.

It remains to prove that
\bar{c}_{u}(P)=\bar{c}_{u}[P]+\bar{c}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}
, i.e.,, for every
i\in[\ell]
, we have
c_{u}^{i}(P)=c^{i}_{u}[P]+c^{i}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}
. Let
i\in[\ell]
.
From the definition of
c^{i}_{u}(P)
and
c^{i}_{u}[P]
, we have

c^{i}_{u}(P)=\lvert[\![X_{i}]\!]^{(G,\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|\mathsf{subtree}[u]},\mathbf{\hat{g}})}\rvert\text{ and }c^{i}_{u}[P]=\lvert[\![X_{i}]\!]^{(G,\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|\mathsf{subtree}(u)},\mathbf{\hat{g}})}\rvert.

If
X_{i}
is an edge set variable, then
c^{i}_{u}(P)=c^{i}_{u}[P]=\lvert\mathbf{\hat{g}}(X_{i})\rvert
while
c^{i}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}=0
holds so the claim follows.
Assume now that
X_{i}
is a vertex set variable.
Then, we have

c^{i}_{u}(P)=\lvert\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{i})\cap\mathsf{subtree}[u]\rvert=\lvert\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{i})\cap\mathsf{subtree}(u)\rvert+\lvert\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{i})\cap\{u\}\rvert=c^{i}_{u}[P]+\lvert\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{i})\cap\{u\}\rvert.

Since
P\in\mathsf{Part}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}]
, we know that
\mathbf{f}_{u}
is the restriction of
\mathbf{\hat{f}}
on
\{u\}
.
Thus, we have
\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{i})\cap\{u\}=\mathbf{f}_{u}(X_{i})
.
By definition, we have
c^{i}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}=\lvert\mathbf{f}_{u}(X_{i})\rvert
.
We conclude that
c^{i}_{u}(P)=c^{i}_{u}[P]+c^{i}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}
.
∎

We are ready to prove (
8
).
By definition, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{u}(I)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b\in\mathbb{N},\bar{c}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}}\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}(I,a,b,\bar{c})\rvert\cdot\alpha^{a}\cdot\beta^{b}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}}=\sum_{P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}(I)}\alpha^{a_{u}(P)}\cdot\beta^{b_{u}(P)}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}_{u}(P)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{claim:modelcheck:forget:disjointunion}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{J}\\
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}]\end{subarray}}\alpha^{a_{u}(P)}\cdot\beta^{b_{u}(P)}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}_{u}(P)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{claim:modelcheck:forget:coef}}}{{=}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}\\
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}]\end{subarray}}\alpha^{a_{u}[P]+a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}\cdot\beta^{b_{u}[P]}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}_{u}[P]+\bar{c}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}\\
a,b\in\mathbb{N},\bar{c}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}\end{subarray}}\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert\cdot\alpha^{a+a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}\cdot\beta^{b}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}+\bar{c}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}}.

For
J=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}
, from the definition of
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}},a,b,\bar{c}]
, we have:

\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}},a,b,\bar{c}]=\bigcap_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\{T\},a,b,\bar{c}].

For every
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}
, we denote by
\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
the set of all
(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\emptyset,a,b,\bar{c}]
such that for every
(C,i)\in\mathcal{T}
, there is no arc in
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
incoming into
u^{i}
.
Observe that

\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]=\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\emptyset,a,b,\bar{c}]\setminus\left(\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal{T}}\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\{T\},a,b,\bar{c}]\right).

By the the inclusion-exclusion principle (
theorem

3.5
), we deduce that

\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert=\sum_{\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}(-1)^{\lvert\mathcal{T}\rvert}\lvert\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert.

(12)

We will prove the “non-DFT-transformed” equality (
6
) with (
8
), (
12
) and the following claim.

Claim 6.14
.

Let
J=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J},a,b\in\mathbb{N},\bar{c}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
. For every
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}
, there exists a neighborhood function
\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
on
\{u\}
with
(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\lvert\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}\cup\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}},\mathtt{pd}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{F},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert,\text{ and }

(13)

\displaystyle(-1)^{\lvert\mathcal{T}\rvert}

\displaystyle=\prod_{C\in}(-1)^{\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)-\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}(u,C)}.

(14)

Furthermore, for every
(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
, we have

|\{\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}\,:\,\mathtt{alw}_{u}=\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}\}|=\prod_{C\in}\binom{\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)}{\mathtt{alw}_{u}(u,C)}.

(15)

Proof of the claim.

First, we fix an arbitrary
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}
and construct
\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
as follows.
For every
C\in
, let
\mathsf{Alw}(C)
be the set of all
i\in[\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)]
and such that
(C,i)\notin\mathcal{T}
and we let
h_{C}
be a bijection between
\mathsf{Alw}(C)
and
\{1,2,\dots,\lvert\mathsf{Alw}(C)\rvert\}
.
We define
\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
as the neighborhood function on
\{u\}
such that for every
C\in
, we have
\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}(u,C)=\lvert\mathsf{Alw}(C)\rvert
.
Observe that
\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
is indeed a neighborhood function because by construction we have
\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}(u,C)\in[\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)]\subseteq[d_{\varphi}]
.

Let
J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}\cup\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}},\mathtt{pd}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{F})
. It is easy to see that
J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+}\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
because by construction we have
\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}\leqslant\mathbb{E}_{u}
.
Next, we provide a bijection between
\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
and
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+},a,b,\bar{c}]
.
For this, for every
C\in
we define the bijection
g_{C}
between the set

\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{A}(\vec{G}_{\varphi})\,:\,w\neq z\}\cup\{(z^{1},u^{j})\in\vec{A}(\vec{G}_{\varphi})\,:\,j\in\mathsf{Alw}(C)\}

(16)

and the set

\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{A}(\vec{G}_{\varphi})\,:\,w\neq z\}\cup\{(z^{1},u^{j})\in\vec{A}(\vec{G}_{\varphi})\,:\,j\in[\mathsf{Alw}(C)]\}

(17)

via

g_{C}((z^{1},w^{j}))=\begin{cases}(z^{1},w^{j})&\text{if }w\neq u\\
(z^{1},u^{h_{C}(j)})&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let
h
be the function that maps every
P=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})\in\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
to
h(P)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{Z}})
where

\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{g_{C}(a)\,:\,a\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\}

for every
C\in
.
This is well-defined because for every
C\in
and every arc
(v^{1},u^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
, we have
j\in\mathsf{Alw}(C)
.
Indeed, by definition of
\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
, we know that
(C,j)\notin\mathcal{T}
and by
(Respect-broom-

^
E

)
and
(

E

-consistent)
we have
j\in[\hat{\mathbb{E}}(u,C)]=[\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)]
.
In simple words, for every
C\in
,
h
“reorders” the arcs in
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
incoming into
\{u^{j}\,:\,j\in\mathsf{Alw}(C)\}

in such a way that they keep their tail and their heads are now in
\{u^{j}\,:\,j\leqslant\lvert\mathsf{Alw}(C)\rvert=\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}(u,C)\}
.
In particular,
g_{C}
is a bijection between
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
and
\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)
.

We first prove that (1)
h
maps every element of
\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
to an element in
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+},a,b,\bar{c}]
, and (2) every element in
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+},a,b,\bar{c}]
has a preimage under
h
in
\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
.
Later we show that the bijectivity easily follows from these two properties.

Let
P=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})\in\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
and let
h(P)=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{Z}})
.
We need to check that
h(P)\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+},a,b,\bar{c}]
.
First observe that
h(P)
is a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
. This is clear from the construction of
\vec{\mathcal{Z}}
and the fact that
g_{C}
is a bijection between
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
and
\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)
for every
C\in
.
In particular,
(Respect-broom-

^
E

)
holds because
\{1,2,\dots,\lvert\mathsf{Alw}(C)\rvert\}\subseteq[\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)]=[\hat{\mathbb{E}}(u,C)]
for every
C\in
.
Now, we show that
h(P)
is compatible with
J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+}
.
Since
P\in\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]\subseteq\mathsf{Part}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}\cup\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{F},a,b,\bar{c}]
, we only need to check the conditions involving
\vec{\mathcal{Z}}
and
\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
, that is:
(

pd

-consistent)
and
(Respect-

alw

)
.
By construction of
\vec{\mathcal{Z}}
, for every
v\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
and
C\in
, we have

\mathtt{pd}(v,C)=\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}}(v,C)=\lvert\{(w^{1},v^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\}\rvert=\lvert\{(w^{1},v^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)\}\rvert=\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{Z}}}(v,C),

where the second equality holds because
g_{C}
is a bijection between
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
and
\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)
that maps, for every vertex
w
of
G
, all arcs incoming into
w^{1},\dots,w^{d_{\varphi}}
to arcs incoming into vertices
w^{1},\dots,w^{d_{\varphi}}
. Thus,
h(P)
satisfies
(

pd

-consistent)
.
By construction, for every
C\in
and arc
(v^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
with
w\neq u
, we have
(v^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)
. The tuple
P
satisfies
(Respect-

alw

)
on
\mathsf{tail}(u)
with the function
\mathtt{alw}\cup\mathbb{E}_{u}
so
h(P)
satisfies
(Respect-

alw

)
on
\mathsf{tail}(u)
with the function
\mathtt{alw}\cup\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
.
Moreover, for every
C\in
and every arc
(v^{1},u^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)
, we have
j\leqslant[\mathsf{Alw}(C)]=\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}(u,C)
by construction. Clearly,
a_{u}[P]=a_{u}[h(P)]
and
\bar{c}_{u}[P]=\bar{c}_{u}[h(P)]
. Moreover, since the number of arcs incoming into
v^{1},\dots,v^{d_{\varphi}}
is the same in
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
and
\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)
for every
C\in
, we have
b_{u}[P]=b_{u}[h(P)]
.
We deduce that
h(P)\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+},a,b,\bar{c}]
.

Let
P^{\prime}=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{Z}})\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+},a,b,\bar{c}]
. Let
\vec{\mathcal{W}}
be the function that maps every
C\in
to the set
\{g_{C}^{-1}(a)\,:\,a\in\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)\}
.
This is well-defined because for every
(v^{1},u^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)
, we have
j\leqslant\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}(u,C)=[\mathsf{Alw}(C)]
due to
(Respect-

alw

)
, so
g_{C}^{-1}((v^{1},u^{j}))=(v^{1},u^{h_{c}^{-1}(j)})
exists and
g_{C}
is a bijection between
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
and
\vec{\mathcal{Z}}(C)
.
We claim that
P\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})\in\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
.
With the same arguments used above, we can prove that
P
is a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
, we have
a_{u}[P^{\prime}]=a_{u}[P]
,
b_{u}[P^{\prime}]=b_{u}[P]
,
\bar{c}_{u}[P^{\prime}]=\bar{c}_{u}[P]
and
P
satisfies all the conditions to be compatible with
(\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}\cup\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{F})
at the exception of
(Respect-

alw

)
.
This latter condition is satisfied by
P
for
\mathsf{tail}(u)
because
P^{\prime}
satisfies
(Respect-

alw

)
for
\mathsf{tail}(u)
and the function
\mathtt{alw}\cup\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
, and for
\{u\}
it is satisfied because
P^{\prime}
satisfies
(Respect-broom-

^
E

)
and it satisfies
(

E

-consistent)
for
\mathbb{E}_{u}
. We deduce that
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}\cup\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{F},a,b,\bar{c}]
.
Furthermore, observe that by construction, for every
C\in
and arc
(v^{1},u^{i})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
, we have
i\in\mathsf{Alw}(C)
, i.e.,
(C,i)\notin\mathcal{T}
. It follows that

P\in\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
, and, by the construction of
\vec{\mathcal{W}}
, it holds that
h(P)=P^{\prime}
.

Finally, recall that
g_{C}
is a bijection between (
16
) and (
17
) for every
C\in
.
Thus
h
is injective and every partial solution in
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+},a,b,\bar{c}]
has at most one preimage in
h
.
Altogether, we obtain that
h
is a bijection between
\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]
and
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J_{\mathcal{T}}^{+},a,b,\bar{c}]
.
We conclude that (
13
) is true.

Observe that (
14
) simply follows from the following equation:

\lvert\mathcal{T}\rvert=\sum_{C\in}\lvert\bigl\{(C,i)\in\mathcal{T}\,:\,i\in[\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)]\bigr\}\rvert=\sum_{C\in}\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)-\lvert\mathsf{Alw}(C)\rvert=\sum_{C\in}\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)-\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}(u,C).

Finally, to prove (
8
), observe that for every neighborhood function
\mathtt{alw}_{u}
on
\{u\}
such that
\mathtt{alw}_{u}\leqslant\mathbb{E}_{u}
and every
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}
, we have
\mathtt{alw}_{u}=\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
if and only if
\lvert\mathcal{T}(C)\rvert=\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)-\mathtt{alw}_{u}(u,C)
for every
C\in
.
Since
\mathcal{T}(C)\subseteq[\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)]
, there are
\binom{\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)}{\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)-\mathtt{alw}_{u}(u,C)}=\binom{\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)}{\mathtt{alw}_{u}(u,C)}
possibilities for each
\mathcal{T}(C)
so that
\mathtt{alw}_{u}=\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}
holds.
∎

In the following, given
J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
, we define the tuple

J^{\prime+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{pd}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{F}).

Thanks to
6.14
, we can reformulate
\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert
as follows.

\displaystyle\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:forget:inclusion:exclusion}}}{{=}}\sum_{\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}(-1)^{\lvert\mathcal{T}\rvert}\lvert\overline{\mathsf{Part}}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert

(18)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{claim:modelcheck:forget:cT}}}{{=}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{alw}_{u}:\\
J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}\\
\mathtt{alw}_{u}=\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\lvert\mathcal{T}\rvert}\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J^{\prime+},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:forget:-1}}}{{=}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{alw}_{u}:\\
J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}\\
\mathtt{alw}_{u}=\mathtt{alw}_{\mathcal{T}}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{C\in}(-1)^{\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)-\mathtt{alw}_{u}(u,C)}\right)\cdot\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J^{\prime+},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:number-of-cT'-with-the-same-allow}}}{{=}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{alw}_{u}:\\
J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{C\in}(-1)^{\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)-\mathtt{alw}_{u}(u,C)}\cdot\binom{\mathbb{E}_{u}(u,C)}{\mathtt{alw}_{u}(u,C)}\right)\cdot\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J^{\prime+},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{alw}_{u}:\\
J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}S_{\pm}(\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u})\cdot\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J^{\prime+},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert

(19)

We are now ready to prove (
6
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{u}(I)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:forget:cT}}}{{=}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}\\
a,b\in\mathbb{N},\bar{c}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}\end{subarray}}\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{E}_{u}},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert\cdot\alpha^{a+a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}\cdot\beta^{b}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}+\bar{c}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:forget:alwu}}}{{=}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}\\
a,b\in\mathbb{N},\bar{c}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{alw}_{u}:\\
J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}S_{\pm}(\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u})\cdot\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[J^{\prime+},a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert\cdot\alpha^{a+a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}\cdot\beta^{b}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}+\bar{c}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}S_{\pm}(\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u})\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{pd}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{F}]\cdot\alpha^{a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}}.

Now we will use this result to prove the “DFT-transformed” equality (
7
).
In the following, given
J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
, we denote by
M(J^{\prime})
the product
S_{\pm}(\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u})\cdot\alpha^{a_{\mathbb{E}_{u}}}\cdot\gamma^{\bar{c}_{\mathbf{f}_{u}}}
.
We remind the reader that we aim at proving that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{u}(I)\equiv_{p}\sum_{J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}M(J^{\prime})\cdot\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{||}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega(p)^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\cdot\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{pd}\cup\hat{q},\mathtt{F}]

for every prime number
p
such that
\mathbb{F}^{*}_{p}
admits a
p_{\varphi}
-th root of unity. For the remainder of the proof, we fix such an arbitrary prime number
p
.
In the following
\equiv
denotes
\equiv_{p}
and
\omega
denotes
\omega(p)
.
We have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{u}(I)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:def-q}}}{{\equiv}}\sum_{q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))}\omega^{q\cdot\mathtt{pd}}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},q,\mathtt{F})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:forget-node-non-transformed}}}{{\equiv}}\sum_{q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))}\omega^{q\cdot\mathtt{pd}}\cdot\Bigl(\sum_{J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}M(J^{\prime})\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},q\cup\mathtt{pd}_{u},\mathtt{F}]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{J^{\prime}=(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}M(J^{\prime})\cdot\sum_{q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))}\Bigl(\omega^{q\cdot\mathtt{pd}}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},q\cup\mathtt{pd}_{u},\mathtt{F}]\Bigr)

Now to prove (
7
), it suffices to show that for each
(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
, we have

\sum_{q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))}\omega^{q\cdot\mathtt{pd}}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},q\cup\mathtt{pd}_{u},\mathtt{F}]\equiv\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{||}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\cdot\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{pd}\cup\hat{q},\mathtt{F}].

(20)

Let
(\mathbf{f}_{u},\mathbb{E}_{u},\mathtt{alw}_{u},\mathtt{pd}_{u})\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
.
To show this, we start by rewriting the right-hand side of this equality which we denote by
\mathsf{RHS}
.
We rely on the fact that
\mathsf{tail}[u]=\mathsf{tail}(u)\cup\{u\}
implies that

\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])=\{\tilde{q}\,:\,\tilde{q}\colon\mathsf{tail}[u]\times\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}\}=\{\tilde{q}_{u}\cup q\,:\,\tilde{q}_{u}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\}),q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))\}.

(21)

Then we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{RHS}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{\equiv_{p}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{||}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\sum_{\tilde{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])}\omega^{\tilde{q}\cdot(\mathtt{pd}\cup\hat{q})}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},\tilde{q},\mathtt{F}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:rewriting-pdegdom}}}{{\equiv_{p}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{||}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{q}_{u}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\}),\\
q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))\end{subarray}}\omega^{(q\cup\tilde{q}_{u})\cdot(\mathtt{pd}\cup\hat{q})}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},q\cup\tilde{q}_{u},\mathtt{F}]

\displaystyle\equiv_{p}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{||}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{q}_{u}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\}),\\
q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))\end{subarray}}\omega^{q\cdot\mathtt{pd}}\cdot\omega^{\tilde{q}_{u}\cdot\hat{q}}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},q\cup\tilde{q}_{u},\mathtt{F}]

\displaystyle\equiv_{p}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{||}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\sum_{\tilde{q}_{u}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{\tilde{q}_{u}\cdot\hat{q}}\sum_{q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))}\omega^{q\cdot\mathtt{pd}}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},q\cup\tilde{q}_{u},\mathtt{F}]

Now we define the function
\Delta\colon\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})\to\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha,\beta,\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{\ell}]

via

\Delta(\tilde{q}_{u})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))}\omega^{q\cdot\mathtt{pd}}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},q\cup\tilde{q}_{u},\mathtt{F}]\pmod{p}.

By inserting this definition in the above equality we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{RHS}\equiv_{p}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{||}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\sum_{\tilde{q}_{u}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{\tilde{q}_{u}\cdot\hat{q}}\cdot\Delta(\tilde{q}_{u})

\displaystyle\equiv_{p}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{||}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\cdot\bigl(\mathsf{DFT}_{\{u\}\times,p_{\varphi},p}(\Delta)\bigr)(\hat{q})

\displaystyle\equiv_{p}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{\varphi}^{|\{u\}\times|}}\sum_{\hat{q}\in\mathtt{PD}(\{u\})}\omega^{-\hat{q}\cdot\mathtt{pd}_{u}}\cdot\bigl(\mathsf{DFT}_{\{u\}\times,p_{\varphi},p}(\Delta)\bigr)(\hat{q})

\displaystyle\equiv_{p}

\displaystyle\Bigl(\mathsf{DFT}^{-1}_{\{u\}\times,p_{\varphi},p}\bigl(\mathsf{DFT}_{\{u\}\times,p_{\varphi},p}(\Delta)\bigr)\Bigr)(\mathtt{pd}_{u})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{thm:inverse-dft-polynomials}}}{{\equiv_{p}}}

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathtt{pd}_{u})\equiv_{p}\sum_{q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}(u))}\omega^{q\cdot\mathtt{pd}}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f}_{u}^{+},\mathbb{E}_{u}^{+},\mathtt{alw}_{u}^{+},q\cup\mathtt{pd}_{u},\mathtt{F}]

proving the desired equality (
20
) and thus concluding the proof.
∎

6.5
Join equality

And finally we prove the equality relating the partial solutions of
\mathsf{tss}[u]
and of
\mathsf{tss}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{tss}(v_{t})
for a node
u
having children
v_{1},\dots,v_{t}
.
For an index
I=(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},\mathtt{pd},\mathtt{F})\in\mathsf{Index}[u]
and an assignment
\mathtt{pd}^{\prime}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])
we define the index
I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}^{\prime}]=(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},\mathtt{pd}^{\prime},\mathtt{F})\in\mathsf{Index}[u]
.

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
u
be an internal node of
T
with children
v_{1},\dots,v_{t}
.
Then we have

\mathcal{P}_{u}[I]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{pd}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{pd}_{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\colon\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1}+\dots+\mathtt{pd}_{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}])

(22)

and

\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{u}[I]\equiv_{p}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{v_{i}}(I)

(23)

for every
I=(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},\mathtt{pd},\mathtt{F})\in\mathsf{Index}[u]
.

Proof.

We start by proving the “non-transformed” equality (
22
).
So let
I=(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},\mathtt{pd},\mathtt{F})\in\mathsf{Index}[u]
be an arbitrary but fixed index.
First, we rewrite both sides of the desired equality.
For the right-hand side we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{pd}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{pd}_{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\colon\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1}+\dots+\mathtt{pd}_{t}\equiv_{\varphi}\mathtt{pd}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}])=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{pd}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{pd}_{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\colon\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1}+\dots+\mathtt{pd}_{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{a,b,\bar{c}\in\mathbb{N}}|\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a,b,\bar{c})|\alpha^{a}\beta^{b}\gamma^{\bar{c}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathbb{N},\\
b_{1},\dots,b_{t}\in\mathbb{N},\\
\bar{c}_{1},\dots,\bar{c}_{t}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell},\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{pd}_{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\colon\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1}+\dots+\mathtt{pd}_{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}|\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})|\alpha^{a_{i}}\beta^{b_{i}}\gamma^{\bar{c}_{i}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathbb{N},\\
b_{1},\dots,b_{t}\in\mathbb{N},\\
\bar{c}_{1},\dots,\bar{c}_{t}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell},\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{pd}_{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\colon\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1}+\dots+\mathtt{pd}_{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd}\end{subarray}}\alpha^{\sum_{i=1}^{t}a_{i}}\beta^{\sum_{i=1}^{t}b_{i}}\gamma^{\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bar{c}_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}|\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})|=

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b\in\mathbb{N},\bar{c}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}}\alpha^{a}\beta^{b}\gamma^{\bar{c}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1}+\dots+a_{t}=a,\\
b_{1}+\dots+b_{t}=b,\\
\bar{c}_{1}+\dots+\bar{c}_{t}=\bar{c},\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{pd}_{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\colon\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1}+\dots+\mathtt{pd}_{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}|\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})|

where
a_{1}+\dots+a_{t}=a
is a shortcut for
a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathbb{N}\colon\sum_{i=1}^{t}a_{i}=a
, also
b_{1}+\dots+b_{t}=b
is a shortcut for
b_{1},\dots,b_{t}\in\mathbb{N}\colon\sum_{i=1}^{t}b_{i}=b
, and
\bar{c}_{1}+\dots+\bar{c}_{t}=\bar{c}
is a shortcut for
\bar{c}_{1},\dots,\bar{c}_{t}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}\colon\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bar{c}_{i}=\bar{c}
.
And the left-hand side of (
22
) can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{u}[I]=\sum_{a,b\in\mathbb{N},\bar{c}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}}\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert\cdot\alpha^{a}\beta^{b}\gamma^{\bar{c}}.

So to prove (
22
), it suffices to show that we have:

\lvert\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]\rvert=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1}+\dots+a_{t}=a,\\
b_{1}+\dots+b_{t}=b,\\
\bar{c}_{1}+\dots+\bar{c}_{t}=\bar{c},\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{pd}_{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\colon\\
\mathtt{pd}_{1}+\dots+\mathtt{pd}_{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}|\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})|

(24)

for all
a,b\in\mathbb{N},{\bar{c}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
.
To obtain the desired equality, we fix arbitrary
a,b\in\mathbb{N},{\bar{c}}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
, define the set

\displaystyle\mathsf{RHS}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Bigl\{

\displaystyle(P_{1},\dots,P_{t})\,:\,

\displaystyle\exists\mathtt{pd}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{pd}_{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u]),a_{1},\dots,a_{t},b_{1},\dots,b_{t}\in\mathbb{N},\bar{c}_{1},\dots,\bar{c}_{t}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}\colon

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathtt{pd}_{i}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd},\sum_{i=1}^{t}a_{i}=a,\sum_{i=1}^{t}b_{i}=b,\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bar{c}_{i}=\bar{c},

\displaystyle P_{i}\in\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c})\text{ for every }i\in[t]\Bigr\}.

Observe that by
definition

5.13
and
definition

5.15
the sets
\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}^{\prime}],a^{\prime},b^{\prime},\bar{c}^{\prime})
and
\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}^{\prime\prime}],a^{\prime\prime},b^{\prime\prime},\bar{c}^{\prime\prime})
are disjoint for every pair
(\mathtt{pd}^{\prime},a^{\prime},b^{\prime},\bar{c}^{\prime})\neq(\mathtt{pd}^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\prime},b^{\prime\prime},\bar{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\times\mathbb{N}^{\ell+2}
and every
i\in[t]
.
Therefore, the right-hand side of (
24
) is equal to the cardinality of
\mathsf{RHS}
.
So to prove (
24
) it remains to provide an injective mapping from
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
to
\mathsf{RHS}
as well as an injective mapping from
\mathsf{RHS}
to
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
.

Observe that the tail of each triad in
\mathsf{tss}[u],\mathsf{tss}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{tss}(v_{t})
is equal to
\mathsf{tail}[u]
, the sets
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})
partition the set
\mathsf{sheaf}(u)
, the sets
\mathsf{subtree}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{subtree}(v_{t})
partition the set
\mathsf{subtree}(u)
, and we have
\mathsf{broom}[u]=\cup_{i\in[t]}\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
.
We will rely on these properties in the following.

Claim 6.16
.

There exists an injective mapping
h_{1}
from
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
to
\mathsf{RHS}
.

Proof of the claim.

For the one direction, we map every partial solution
(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})
in
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
to the tuple

h_{1}(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})=(P_{1},\dots,P_{t})

where for every
i\in[t]
,
P_{i}=(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i},\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i},\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i})
such that

•

\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}
is the restriction
\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|_{\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]}}
of
\mathbf{\hat{f}}
on
\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
,

•

\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}
is the restriction
\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{|_{\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})}}
of
\mathbf{\hat{g}}
on
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
,

•

\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
is the restriction
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{|_{\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]}}
of
\hat{\mathbb{E}}
on
\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
,

•

and
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
is the restriction
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{|_{\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})}}
of
\vec{\mathcal{W}}
to
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
.

Before showing that this is indeed a mapping into
\mathsf{RHS}
, let us start with injectivity since it is simpler.
For this we consider two elements
P=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})
and
Q=(\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime},\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{\prime},\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime},\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime})
in
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
such that
h_{1}(P)=h_{1}(Q)
.
This implies the following:

•

For every
X\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)\cap\mathsf{broom}[u]=\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)\cap(\mathsf{broom}[v_{1}]\cup\dots\cup\mathsf{broom}[v_{t}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)\cap\mathsf{broom}[v_{1}])\cup\dots\cup(\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)\cap\mathsf{broom}[v_{t}])=\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{1}(X)\cup\dots\cup\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{t}(X)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{1}(P_{1})=h_{1}(P_{2})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime}_{1}(X)\cup\dots\cup\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime}_{t}(X)=(\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime}(X)\cap\mathsf{broom}[v_{1}])\cup\dots\cup(\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime}(X)\cap\mathsf{broom}[v_{t}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime}(X)\cap(\mathsf{broom}[v_{1}]\cup\dots\cup\mathsf{broom}[v_{t}])=\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime}(X)\cap\mathsf{broom}[u]=\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime}(X).

Hence
\mathbf{\hat{f}}=\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{\prime}
.

•

For every
Y\in\mathsf{var}^{E}(\varphi)
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(u)=\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)\cap(\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1})\cup\dots\cup\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1}))\cup\dots\cup(\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t}))=\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{1}(Y)\cup\dots\cup\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{t}(Y)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{1}(P_{1})=h_{1}(P_{2})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{2}(Y)\cup\dots\cup\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{t}^{\prime}(Y)=(\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{\prime}(Y)\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1}))\cup\dots\cup(\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{\prime}(Y)\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{\prime}(Y)\cap(\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1})\cup\dots\cup\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t}))=\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{\prime}(Y)\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(u)=\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{\prime}(Y).

Hence
\mathbf{\hat{g}}=\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{\prime}
.

•

For every vertex
z\in\mathsf{subtree}(u)
there exists a (unique) index
i\in[t]
with
z\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]
. Then for every
C\in
we have

\hat{\mathbb{E}}(z,C)=\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(z,C)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{1}(P_{1})=h_{1}(P_{2})}}{{=}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{i}(z,C)=\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}(z,C).

And for every vertex
z\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
and every
C\in
we have
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(z,C)=\mathbb{E}(z,C)=\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}(z,C)
since both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
satisfy
(

E

-consistent)
for
I
.
So we get
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{1}=\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{2}
.

•

And for every
C\in
we have

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)=

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\cap\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(u)=\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\cap(\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{1})\cup\dots\cup\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{t}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\cap\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{1}))\cup\dots\cup(\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\cap\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{t}))=\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{1}(C)\cup\dots\cup\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{t}(C)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{1}(P_{1})=h_{1}(P_{2})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{1}(C)\cup\dots\cup\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{t}(C)=(\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}(C)\cap\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{1}))\cup\dots\cup(\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}(C)\cap\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{t}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}(C)\cap(\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{1})\cup\dots\cup\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{t}))=\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}(C)\cap\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(u)=\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}(C)

Hence,
\vec{\mathcal{W}}=\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
.

Altogether, we obtain
P_{1}=P_{2}
implying the injectivity of
h_{1}
.

It remains to show that for every
P=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
, its image
h_{1}(P)
belongs to
\mathsf{RHS}
.
For this, we first define the following values for every
i\in[t]
and every
j\in[\ell]
:

a_{i}=\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\colon\\
\mathbb{E}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C),

b_{i}=\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

c^{j}_{i}=\begin{cases}\lvert\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\rvert&\text{if }X_{j}\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)\\
\lvert\mathbf{\hat{g}}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})\rvert&\text{if }X_{j}\in\mathsf{var}^{E}(\varphi)\\
\end{cases}.

For
i\in[t]
, we then define the vector
\bar{c}_{i}=(c_{i}^{1},\dots,c_{i}^{\ell})\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
.
We then also have the following for every
i\in[t]
and every
j\in[\ell]
:

a_{i}=\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\colon\\
\mathbb{E}_{i}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(v,C),

(25)

b_{i}=\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

(26)

c^{j}_{i}=\begin{cases}\lvert\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\rvert&\text{if }X_{j}\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)\\
\lvert\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}(X_{j})\rvert&\text{if }X_{j}\in\mathsf{var}^{E}(\varphi)\\
\end{cases}.

(27)

Finally, for every
i\in[t]
we define the mapping
\mathtt{pd}_{i}\colon\mathsf{tail}(v_{i})\times\to\mathbb{F}_{p_{\varphi}}
to be equal to
\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}
.

We then have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}a_{i}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\colon\\
\mathbb{E}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)=\sum_{C\in}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\colon\\
\mathbb{E}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{subtree}(u)=\mathsf{subtree}[v_{1}]\dot{\cup}\dots\dot{\cup}\mathsf{subtree}[v_{t}]}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}(u)\colon\\
\mathbb{E}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)=a_{u}[P]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]}}{{=}}a

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}b_{i}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{W}_{i}(C)\subseteq\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{i}),\\
V(\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i}))\subseteq\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]\subseteq\mathsf{broom}[u]\end{subarray}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[u]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[u]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{sheaf}(u)=\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1})\dot{\cup}\dots\dot{\cup}\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[u]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\}\rvert=b_{u}[P]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]}}{{=}}b.

Also for
j\in[\ell]
, if
X_{j}\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)
, we have:

\sum_{i=1}^{t}c_{i}^{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\lvert\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\rvert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{subtree}(u)=\mathsf{subtree}[v_{1}]\dot{\cup}\dots\dot{\cup}\mathsf{subtree}[v_{t}]}}{{=}}\lvert\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{subtree}(u)\rvert=c^{j}_{u}[P]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]}}{{=}}c^{j}

and if
X_{j}\in\mathsf{var}^{E}(\varphi)
, we have

\sum_{i=1}^{t}c_{i}^{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\lvert\mathbf{\hat{g}}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})\rvert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{sheaf}(u)=\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1})\dot{\cup}\dots\dot{\cup}\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})}}{{=}}\lvert\mathbf{\hat{g}}(X_{j})\rvert=c^{j}_{u}[P]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]}}{{=}}c^{j}.

We recall that we have
\mathsf{tail}[u]=\mathsf{tail}(v_{i})
for every
i\in[t]
.
Then the following holds for every
w\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
and every
C\in
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathtt{pd}_{i}(w,C)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}(w,C)\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}|\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{sheaf}(u)=\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1})\dot{\cup}\dots\dot{\cup}\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle|\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]}}{{\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}}}\mathtt{pd}(w,C).

Therefore, we get
\mathtt{pd}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd}_{1}+\dots+\mathtt{pd}_{t}
.

It thus remains to show that for every
i\in[t]
, the tuple
P_{i}=(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i},\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i},\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i})
belongs to the set
\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})
.
So let
i\in[t]
be arbitrary but fixed.
First, we show that
P_{i}
is a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}(v_{i})=(\mathsf{tail}(v_{i}),\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}],\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i}))
.
For this we will strongly rely on the fact that
P
is a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
.
By definition,
P_{i}
is
(Domain-respecting)
.
Let now
C=(X,Y)\in
be arbitrary but fixed:

•

(Edge-consistent)
: Consider an arbitrary edge
zw\in\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
. Note that we then have
w\in\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
.
Then there exists
j\in[d_{\varphi}]
with
(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(X,Y)
if and only if there exists
j\in[d_{\varphi}]
with
(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(X,Y)
—this is because
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{q})
is disjoint from
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
for every
q\neq i\in[t]
.
Since
P
is a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
this is equivalent to
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)
and
zw\in\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)
. We have
z\in\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
and
zw\in\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
so this is equivalent to
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X)
and
zw\in\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}(Y)
.

•

(Respect-subtree-

^
E

)
: Consider an arbitrary vertex
z\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\subseteq\mathsf{subtree}(u)
and arbitrary
j\in[\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(v,C)]=[\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)]
.
Partial solution
P
having this property for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
implies that
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
contains an arc
(w^{1},z^{j})
for some vertex
w
. By construction of the digraph
\vec{G}_{\varphi}
,
wz
is then an edge of
G
.
Every edge of
G
incident with
z
in
G
belongs to
\mathsf{sheaf}(z)\subseteq\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
.
Therefore, the arc
(w^{1},z^{j})
is also contained in
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\cap\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{i})=\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
.

•

(Respect-broom-

^
E

)
: Due to
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
, for every
v\in\mathsf{broom}[u]
, the set
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
does not contain any arc incoming into
v^{j}
for any
j>\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)
.
Then this property holds for the subset
\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
of
\mathsf{broom}[u]
, the subset
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
of
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
, and the restriction
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
of
\mathbb{E}
to
\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
for every
i\in[t]
.

So
P_{i}
is indeed a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}(v_{i})
.

Next, we show that
P_{i}
is compatible with the index
I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}]=(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},\mathtt{pd}_{i},\mathtt{F})
for
\mathsf{tss}(v_{i})
. We will rely on the fact that
P
is compatible with
I
for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
.
The properties
(

f

-consistent)
and
(

E

-consistent)
follow directly from
\mathsf{tail}[u]=\mathsf{tail}(v_{i})
.
The property
(

pd

-consistent)
holds by definition of
\mathtt{pd}_{i}
.
The property
(Respect-

alw

)
follows from
\mathsf{tail}[u]=\mathsf{tail}(v_{i})
as well as
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\subseteq\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
for every
C\in
.
It remains to show that
(Respect-

F

)
is satisfied.
Recall that
P
is compatible with
I
so we have:

\mathbf{\bot}(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}},(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}
.
Since
\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\subseteq\mathsf{subtree}(u)
and
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})\subseteq\mathsf{sheaf}[u]
by
lemma

5.8
we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bot}((\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{|_{\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})}},(\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},((\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}})\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bot}(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}},(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}.

Since
\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\subseteq\mathsf{subtree}(u)
and
\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\subseteq\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
by definition of
P_{i}
this implies

\mathbf{\bot}((\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i},(\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}.

Now we show that
(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}=(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}
holds.
For this consider an arbitrary vertex
w\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]
and arbitrary
C\in
.
By
3.2
,
w
has no incident edges in
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{j})
for any
j\neq i\in[t]
.
Then we have

\displaystyle(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}(w,C)=

\displaystyle\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}}(w,C)=|\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\}|=|\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\cap\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(v_{i})\}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}|=\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}(w,C)=(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}(w,C)

So indeed
(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}=(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}
holds and we get

\mathbf{\bot}((\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i},(\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}.

Altogether we obtain that
P_{i}
is compatible with
I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}]
for
\mathsf{tss}(v_{i})
.
Finally, by (
25
), (
26
), and (
27
), the partial solution
P_{i}
is contained in
\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})
.
Therefore,
h_{1}(P)
indeed belongs to
\mathsf{RHS}
, and
\varphi
is a well-defined mapping from
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
to
\mathsf{RHS}
.
∎

Claim 6.17
.

There exists an injective mapping
h_{2}
from
\mathsf{RHS}
to
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
.

Proof of the claim.

Let
(P_{1},\dots,P_{t})\in\mathsf{RHS}
with
P_{i}=(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i},\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i},\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i})
for every
i\in[t]
.
We define
h_{2}(P_{1},\dots,P_{t})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=P=(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})
such that

•

For every
X\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)
, we have
\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X)
.

•

For every
Y\in\mathsf{var}^{E}(\varphi)
, we have
\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}(Y)
.

•

For every
C\in
and every
z\in\mathsf{subtree}(u)
, let
i\in[t]
be such that
z\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]
.
Then we have
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(z,C)=\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(z,C)
. And for every
C\in
and every
z\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
we let
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(z,C)=\mathbb{E}(z,C)
.

•

For every
C\in
we have
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
.

By definition of
\mathsf{RHS}
there exist
\mathtt{pd}_{i}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])
,
a_{i},b_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\bar{c}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
with
P_{i}\in\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}],a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})
for every
i\in[t]
and
\sum_{i\in[t]}\mathtt{pd}_{i}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\mathtt{pd}
,
\sum_{i\in[t]}a_{i}=a
,
\sum_{i\in[t]}b_{i}=b
, and
\sum_{i\in[t]}\bar{c}_{i}=\bar{c}
.
We denote the index
I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}]
by
I_{i}
.

First we show that
P
is a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
.
Recall that
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})
partition
\mathsf{sheaf}(u)
while
\mathsf{subtree}[v_{1}],\dots,\mathsf{subtree}[v_{t}]
partition
\mathsf{subtree}(u)
.
And we also have
\mathsf{broom}[u]=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
.
We will rely on the fact that
P_{i}
is a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}(v_{i})
for every
i\in[t]
.
First, this implies that
\mathbf{\hat{f}}
is a vertex interpretation on
\mathsf{broom}[u]
and
\mathbf{\hat{g}}
is an edge interpretation on
\mathsf{sheaf}(u)
.
Further,
\hat{\mathbb{E}}
is a well-defined neighborhood function on
\mathsf{broom}[u]
.
We now argue that for every
C\in
, the set
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
is a simple subset of
\vec{\mathsf{sheaf}}(u)
.
For every edge
zw\in E(G)
there exists at most one index
i\in[t]
with
zw\in\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
.
Since
P_{i}
satisfies
(Domain-respecting)
, the set
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
is simple.
Thus,
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
is also simple and
P
satisfies
(Domain-respecting)
.
Now we show that the remaining three conditions are satisfied as well:

•

(Edge-consistent)
: Consider an arbitrary edge
zw\in\mathsf{sheaf}(u)
and let
i\in[t]
be unique such that
zw\in\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
.
Then we have
z\in\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
.
Further, let
(X,Y)\in
be arbitrary.
Then there exists
j\in[d_{\varphi}]
with
(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(X,Y)
if and only if there exists
j\in[d_{\varphi}]
with
(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(X,Y)
. Since
P_{i}
is a partial solution of
\mathsf{tss}(v_{i})
this is equivalent to
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X)
and
zw\in\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}(Y)

First, we show that
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X)
is equivalent to
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)
.
The property
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X)
trivially implies
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)
.
On the other hand, let
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)
.
Then by definition there exists an index
j\in[t]
with
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{j}(X)
.
Now if
z\in\mathsf{tail}(v_{j})
, since
P_{j}
satisfies
(

f

-consistent)
for
\mathbf{f}
, we have
z\in\mathbf{f}(X)
, and since
P_{i}
satisfies
(

f

-consistent)
for
\mathbf{f}
, we, in turn, get
z\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X)
.
And if
z\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{j}]
, we have
i=j
as otherwise
\mathsf{subtree}[v_{j}]
and
\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]
are disjoint.

Since we have
zw\in\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})
and the sets
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})
are pairwise disjoint, the property
zw\in\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}(Y)
is equivalent to
\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)
.
So
(Edge-consistent)
is satisfied.

•

(Respect-subtree-

^
E

)
:
Consider an arbitrary vertex
z\in\mathsf{subtree}(u)
, an arbitrary
C\in
, and an arbitrary index
j\in[\hat{\mathbb{E}}(z,C)]
.
Let
i\in[t]
be the unique index such that
z\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]
.
By definition of
\hat{\mathbb{E}}
we then have
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(z,C)=\hat{\mathbb{E}}(z,C)
and therefore,
j\in[\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(z,C)]
.
Since
P_{i}
is a partial solution of
\mathsf{tss}(v_{i})
, the set
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
contains an arc incoming into
z^{j}
so
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
does as well.

•

(Respect-broom-

^
E

)

Consider a vertex
w\in\mathsf{broom}[u]
,
C\in
, and an index
j\in[d_{\varphi}]
with
j>\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)
.
If
w\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
, then for every
i\in[t]
we have
\mathsf{tss}[u]=\mathsf{tail}(v_{i})
and thus
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(u,C)=\mathbb{E}(u,C)=\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(w)
(since
P_{i}
satisfies
(

E

-consistent)
for
I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}]
).
As
P_{i}
also satisfies
(Respect-broom-

^
E

)
, the set
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
does not contain any arc incoming into
w^{j}
.
And if
w\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]
, then first, we have
w\notin\mathsf{broom}[v_{q}]
for every
q\neq i\in[t]
, and thus
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{q}(C)
contains no arcs incoming into
w^{j}
; and second, we have
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)=\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(w,C)
and since
P_{i}
satisfies
(Respect-broom-

^
E

)
with respect to
I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}_{i}]
, the set
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
also contains no such arcs.
So in both cases
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
contains no arcs incoming into
w^{j}
.

Altogether,
P
is a partial solution for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
.

Next we show that
P
is compatible with the index
I=(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},\mathtt{pd},\mathtt{F})
for
\mathsf{tss}[u]
.
First, for
(

f

-consistent)
, for every
X\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)
we have

\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)\cap\mathsf{tail}[u]=(\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X))\cap\mathsf{tail}[u]=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X)\cap\mathsf{tail}[u])=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X)\cap\mathsf{tail}(v_{i}))=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{f}_{i}(X)

where the last equality holds since each
P_{i}
satisfies
(

f

-consistent)
for
I_{i}
.

(

E

-consistent)
is satisfied by
P
by construction.
Next, for every
z\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
and every
C\in
we have

\displaystyle\mathtt{pd}(z,C)\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[t]}\mathtt{pd}_{i}(z,C)\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}\sum_{i\in[t]}\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}(z,C)=\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}}(z,C)

where the second equality since
P_{i}
satisfies
(

pd

-consistent)
for
I_{i}
and the least equality holds since
\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{1}(C),\dots,\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{t}(C)
partition
\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
.
So
(

pd

-consistent)
is satisfied by
P
.
Further, for every
z\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
, every
C\in
, and every edge
(w^{1},z^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)
the following holds.
Let
i\in[t]
be such that
(w^{1},z^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)
.
Since
P_{i}
is compatible with
I_{i}
and satisfies
(Respect-

alw

)
, we have
j\leqslant\mathtt{alw}(z,C)
—so this property also applies to
P
.

Finally, we show that the inclusion
\mathbf{\bot}(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}},(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}
holds.
For every
i\in[t]
, since
P_{i}
satisfies
(Respect-

F

)
, for the index
I_{i}
, we have

\mathbf{\bot}((\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i},(\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}.

The following holds since
\mathsf{subtree}[v_{1}],\dots,\mathsf{subtree}[v_{t}]
partition
\mathsf{subtree}(u)
and
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})
partition
\mathsf{sheaf}(u)
:

•

\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}}=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}
,

•

\mathbf{\hat{g}}=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}
,

•

\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}}=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}(\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}
,

•

for every
i\neq j\in[t]
and every vertex
z\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]
, the vertex
z
has no incident edges in
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{j})
so for any
C\in
we have
\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}}(z,C)=\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}(z,C)
.
Hence,
(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}}=\bigcup_{i\in[t]}(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}}
.

So we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bot}(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}},(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}(u)}})=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bot}(\bigcup_{i\in[t]}(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i},\bigcup_{i\in[t]}(\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},\bigcup_{i\in[t]}(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{claim:union-false-set}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\bigcup_{i\in[t]}\mathbf{\bot}((\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i},(\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}},(\mathtt{pd}_{\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}})_{|_{\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]}})\subseteq\mathtt{F}.

Thus
P
satisfies
(Respect-

F

)
and therefore, we have
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I]
.

Next, we show that we have
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
.
First, it holds that

\displaystyle a=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}a_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P_{i}\in\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I_{i},a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}a_{v_{i}}(P_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\colon\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(v,C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C\in}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]\colon\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)=\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathsf{subtree}(u)\colon\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{E}}(v,C)=a_{u}[P].

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle b=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}b_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P_{i}\in\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I_{i},a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}b_{v_{i}}(P_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{subarray}{c}V(\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i}))\subseteq\mathsf{broom}[v_{i}]\end{subarray}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[u]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Def.\ of $\hat{\mathbb{E}}$, }\\
\text{$P_{i}$ compatible with $I_{i}$}\end{subarray}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[u]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[u]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(C)\}\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C\in}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathsf{broom}[u]\\
\hat{\mathbb{E}}(w,C)<d_{\varphi}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{(z^{1},w^{j})\in\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\}\rvert=b_{u}[P]

For every
j\in[\ell]
, if
X_{j}\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)
, then we have

\displaystyle c^{j}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}c^{j}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P_{i}\in\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I_{i},a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}c^{j}_{v_{i}}(P_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{t}|\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{q}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]=\emptyset\text{ for }q\neq i\in[t]}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}|\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]|=|\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X_{j})\cap\mathsf{subtree}(u)|=c^{j}_{u}(P),

and if
X_{j}\in\mathsf{var}^{E}(\varphi)
, then we have

\displaystyle c^{j}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}c^{j}_{i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P_{i}\in\mathsf{Part}_{v_{i}}(I_{i},a_{i},b_{i},\bar{c}_{i})}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{t}c^{j}_{v_{i}}(P_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}(X_{j})=\mathbf{\hat{g}}(X_{j})=c^{j}_{u}(P).

Altogether, we obtain that
P\in\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]=\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
holds and therefore,
h_{2}
is a well-defined mapping from
\mathsf{RHS}
to
\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
.

It remains to show that
h_{2}
is injective.
For this consider
(P^{1}_{1},\dots,P^{1}_{t}),(P^{2}_{1},\dots,P^{2}_{t})\in\mathsf{RHS}
with
h_{2}(P^{1}_{1},\dots,P^{1}_{t})=h_{2}(P^{2}_{1},\dots,P^{2}_{t})
.
And for
i\in[t]
and
j\in[2]
, let
P^{j}_{i}=(\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}^{j},\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}^{j},\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}^{j},\vec{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{j})
.
Also let
(\mathbf{\hat{f}},\mathbf{\hat{g}},\hat{\mathbb{E}},\vec{\mathcal{W}})=h_{2}(P^{1}_{1},\dots,P^{1}_{t})=h_{2}(P^{2}_{1},\dots,P^{2}_{t})
.
Now fix an arbitrary
i\in[t]
.
First, for every vertex
z\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
, every
j\in[2]
, and every
X\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)
, since
P_{i}^{j}
is compatible with
I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}^{\prime}]
for some
\mathtt{pd}^{\prime}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])
, we have
u\in\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{j}_{i}(X)
if and only if
u\in\mathbf{f}(X)
.
Further, and every
X\in\mathsf{var}^{V}(\varphi)
, since
\mathsf{subtree}[v_{1}],\dots,\mathsf{subtree}[v_{t}]
are pairwise disjoint,
we have

\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{1}_{i}(X)\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]=\mathbf{\hat{f}}(X)\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]=\mathbf{\hat{f}}^{2}_{i}(X)\cap\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}].

Hence,
\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}^{1}=\mathbf{\hat{f}}_{i}^{2}
.
Further, for every
Y\in\mathsf{var}^{E}(\varphi)
, since
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})
are pairwise disjoint, we also have

\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{1}_{i}(Y)=\mathbf{\hat{g}}(Y)\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})=\mathbf{\hat{g}}^{2}_{i}(Y),

i.e.,
\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}^{1}=\mathbf{\hat{g}}_{i}^{2}
.
Similarly, for every vertex
z\in\mathsf{tail}[u]
, every
j\in[2]
, and every
C\in
, since
P_{i}^{j}
is compatible with
I[\mathtt{pd}\mapsto\mathtt{pd}^{\prime}]
for some
\mathtt{pd}^{\prime}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])
, we have
\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{i}^{j}(z,C)=\mathbb{E}(z,C)
.
And for every
j\in[2]
,
C\in
, and
z\in\mathsf{subtree}[v_{i}]
we have

\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{1}_{i}(z,C)=\mathbb{E}(z,C)=\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{2}_{i}(z,C),

hence
\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{1}_{i}=\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{2}_{i}
.
And finally, for every
C\in
, since
\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{t})
are pairwise disjoint, we have

\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{1}_{i}(C)=\vec{\mathcal{W}}(C)\cap\mathsf{sheaf}(v_{i})=\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{i}(C),

i.e.,
\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{1}_{i}=\vec{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{i}
.
Altogether, we get
(P^{1}_{1},\dots,P^{1}_{t})=(P^{2}_{1},\dots,P^{2}_{t})
and therefore,
h_{2}
is injective.
∎

The existence of injective mappings
h_{1}\colon\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]\to\mathsf{RHS}
and
h_{2}\colon\mathsf{RHS}\to\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]
implies that we have
|\mathsf{Part}_{u}[I,a,b,\bar{c}]|=|\mathsf{RHS}|
concluding the proof of (
22
).

Now given that this property holds, we will apply the convolution result for DFTs (cf.
theorem

A.4
) to prove the second part of the claim, namely that (
23
) holds for all
I\in\mathsf{Index}[u]
.
Let thus
p
be an arbitrary but fixed prime such that
\mathbb{F}^{*}_{p}
admits a
p_{\varphi}
-th root of unity.
Before we proceed with the formal proof, let us briefly sketch the idea behind it.
If we fix
(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},\mathtt{F})
and only vary
\mathtt{pd}
then
\mathcal{P}_{u}[\cdot]
and
\mathcal{P}_{v_{i}}(\cdot)
(modulo
p
) become functions from
(\mathsf{tail}[u]\times)\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}
to
\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha,\beta,\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{\ell}]
, and
\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{u}[\cdot]
and
\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{v_{i}}(\cdot)
become their discrete Fourier transforms, respectively.
Given this, the convolution result (
theorem

A.4
) directly implies the desired equality (
23
).
Now we provide the formal details.

In the remainder of the proof
\equiv
without any subscript denotes
\equiv_{p}
and
\omega
denotes
\omega(p)
.
Let
I=(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},\mathtt{pd},\mathtt{F})\in\mathsf{Index}[u]
be arbitrary but fixed.
And let
D=\mathsf{tail}[u]\times
.
Recall that we have
\mathsf{tail}[u]=\mathsf{tail}(v_{i})
for every
i\in[t]
.
We define the mappings
h,h^{1},\dots,h^{t},g,g^{1},\dots,g^{t}\colon(D\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}})\to\mathbb{F}_{p}[\alpha,\beta,\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{\ell}]
as:

h(y)\equiv\mathcal{P}_{u}[\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},y,\mathtt{F}]\text{ and }g(y)\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{u}[\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},y,\mathtt{F}]

and

h^{i}(y)\equiv\mathcal{P}_{v_{i}}(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},y,\mathtt{F})\text{ and }g^{i}(y)\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{v_{i}}(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},y,\mathtt{F})

for every
i\in[t]
and every
y\colon D\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}
.

First, the equality (
22
) implies that we have

h(y)\equiv\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}y^{1},\dots,y^{t}\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])\\
y^{1}+\dots+y^{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}y\end{subarray}}h^{1}(y^{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot h^{t}(y^{t})\equiv\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}y^{1},\dots,y^{t}\colon D\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}\\
y^{1}+\dots+y^{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}y\end{subarray}}h^{1}(y^{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot h^{t}(y^{t}).

(28)

Second, by definitions of the polynomials
\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{u}[\cdot]
and
\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{v_{i}}(\cdot)
(cf.
eq.

3
) it holds that

g(y)\equiv\sum_{q\in\mathtt{PD}(\mathsf{tail}[u])}\prod_{(v,C)\in\mathsf{tail}[u]\times}\omega^{q(v,C)\cdot y(v,C)}h(q)\equiv\sum_{q\colon D\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}}\prod_{z\in D}\omega^{q(z)\cdot y(z)}h(q)

and

g^{i}(y)\equiv\sum_{q\colon\mathsf{tail}(v_{i})\times\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}}\prod_{(v,C)\in\mathsf{tail}(v_{i})\times}\omega^{q(v,C)\cdot y(v,C)}h^{i}(q)\equiv\sum_{q\colon D\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}}\prod_{z\in D}\omega^{q(z)\cdot y(z)}h^{i}(q)

for every
y\colon D\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}
and every
i\in[t]
, i.e., we have

g\equiv\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}(h)\text{ and }g^{i}\equiv\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}(h^{i}).

(29)

Then for every
y\colon D\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
we have:

\displaystyle h(y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:important-1}}}{{\equiv}}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}y^{1},\dots,y^{t}\colon D\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}\\
y^{1}+\dots+y^{t}\equiv_{p_{\varphi}}y\end{subarray}}h^{1}(y^{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot h^{t}(y^{t})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:fourier-convolution-result-polynomials}}}}{{\equiv}}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}^{-1}(\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}(h^{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}(h^{t})))(y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:important-2}}}{{\equiv}}(\mathsf{DFT}_{D,{p_{\varphi}},p}^{-1}(g^{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot g^{t}))(y),

i.e.,

h\equiv\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}^{-1}(g^{1}\cdot\dots\cdot g^{t}),

and hence

g\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:important-2}}}{{\equiv}}\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}(h)\equiv\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}(\mathsf{DFT}_{D,p_{\varphi},p}^{-1}(g^{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot g^{t}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:inverse-dft-polynomials}}}}{{\equiv}}g^{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot g^{t}.

(30)

Finally, by definitions of
h,h^{1},\dots,h^{t},g,g^{1},\dots,g^{t}
we obtain

\mathcal{Q}_{u}[\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},y,\mathtt{F}]\equiv g(y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:g-equality-product-g-i-s}}}{{\equiv}}(g^{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot g^{t})(y)\equiv\prod_{i=1}^{t}\mathcal{Q}_{v_{i}}(\mathbf{f},\mathbb{E},\mathtt{alw},y,\mathtt{F})

for every
y\colon D\to\mathbb{Z}_{p_{\varphi}}
concluding the proof of (
23
).
∎

7
Model Checking

Here, we show how given the equalities from the previous section, we can obtain the desired space-efficient algorithm checking whether the input formula
\varphi\in\mathsf{NEO_{2}}[\mathsf{FRec}]
is satisfied by the graph
G
when an elimination tree
T
of depth
\mathsf{td}
is provided with the input.
Directly using those equalities, one could obtain a recursive algorithm with running time single-exponential in
\mathsf{td}
and polynomial space complexity: at each of
\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{td})
levels of the recursion, one would keep the current partial sums and products of polynomials.
Since the number of non-zero monomials is upper-bounded by
\mathcal{O}(n^{\mathcal{O}(\ell)}\cdot d_{\varphi}\cdot\lvert\varphi\rvert)
(cf.
lemma

5.16
) and the length of each coefficient is upper-bounded by

\mathcal{O}(n\cdot\mathsf{td}\cdot|\varphi|\cdot\log(d_{\varphi}))

(cf.
Lemma

6.2
), the polynomial space complexity follows.
In this section, we show how to improve the space complexity to have only logarithmic dependency on
n
and eventually prove our main result.

7.1
Getting to logarithmic space complexity

To improve the space complexity to have only logarithmic dependency on
n
, we will rely on the Chinese remainder theorem which allows us to use the evaluation of the polynomials from the previous subsection in a “small” set of “small” prime fields so that we can reconstruct the bits of the coefficients of the desired “root” polynomial in logarithmic in
n
space.
This idea was first used on elimination trees by Pilipczuk and Wrochna
[
37
]
to solve the
Dominating Set
problem.
In order to ensure that our equalities from the previous subsection hold, we will use the small primes
p
such that, additionally,
\mathbb{F}^{*}_{p}
admits the
p_{\varphi}
-th root of unity.
The idea of combining Chinese remainder theorem with discrete Fourier transforms was used by Focke et al.
[
20
]
to obtain fast convolutions for
(\sigma,\rho)
-
Dominating Set
on graphs of bounded treewidth.
We remark that their algorithm has exponential space complexity.
Now we formalize this sketch.
We first set up the algebraic tools and after that, put everything together to obtain the desired algorithm.

First of all, the approach of Pilipczuk and Wrochna
[
37
]
only works for univariate polynomials so we start by providing a coefficient-preserving transformation of multivariate polynomials of bounded degree into univariate polynomials.
Bergougnoux et al.
[
5
]
carried out this transformation for polynomials in two variables, and we now generalize their idea to our polynomials in
\ell+2
variables.
For this let

\mathsf{A}=2\cdot|\varphi|\cdot d_{\varphi}\cdot n\cdot\mathsf{td}+1,

the choice of
\mathsf{A}
becomes clear in the proof of
lemma

7.6
.
We also define

\mathsf{A}^{*}=\mathsf{A}^{\ell+2}-1.

Now consider an arbitrary polynomial
\mathcal{P}
in
\ell+2
formal variables
\alpha,\beta,\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{\ell}
of form

\mathcal{P}=\sum_{0\leqslant a,b,c_{1},\dots,c_{\ell}<\mathsf{A}}q_{a,b,\bar{c}}\alpha^{a}\beta^{b}\gamma^{\bar{c}}\in\mathbb{Z}[\alpha,\beta,\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{\ell}],

i.e., the degree of every variable is upper-bounded by
\mathsf{A}
(in the above,
\bar{c}=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{\ell})
).
Then we define the univariate polynomial
\mathcal{P}^{\uparrow}\in\mathbb{Z}[x]
as

\mathcal{P}^{\uparrow}=\sum_{0\leqslant a,b,c_{1},\dots,c_{\ell}\leqslant\mathsf{A}}q_{a,b,\bar{c}}x^{a}\Bigl(\prod_{i\in[\ell]}x^{c_{i}\cdot\mathsf{A}^{i}}\Bigr)x^{b\cdot\mathsf{A}^{\ell+1}}=\sum_{0\leqslant a,b,c_{1},\dots,c_{\ell}\leqslant\mathsf{A}}q_{a,b,\bar{c}}x^{a+\sum_{i\in[\ell]}c_{i}\cdot\mathsf{A}^{i}+b\cdot\mathsf{A}^{\ell+1}}.

The following properties are crucial.

Observation 7.1
.

The sum in exponent of each monomial in
\mathcal{P}^{\uparrow}
is the representation of some number between
0
and
\mathsf{A}^{*}
to the base
\mathsf{A}
.
Furthermore, every number has a unique base
\mathsf{A}
representation.
Thus the following hold:

•

The degree of
\mathcal{P}^{\uparrow}
is at most
\mathsf{A}^{*}
.

•

For every exponent
0\leqslant j\leqslant\mathsf{A}^{*}
, the coefficient at
x^{j}
in
\mathcal{P}^{\uparrow}
is precisely
q_{a,b,\bar{c}}
for the unique representation
j=a+\Bigl(\sum_{i\in[\ell]}c_{i}\cdot\mathsf{A}^{i}\Bigr)+b\cdot\mathsf{A}^{\ell+1}
of
j
to the base
\mathsf{A}
—hence, the task of reconstructing the coefficients of
\mathcal{P}
is equivalent to the one of
\mathcal{P}^{\uparrow}
.

•

For any
s\in\mathbb{Z}
and for the evaluation of
\mathcal{P}^{\uparrow}
at
s
, it holds that

\mathcal{P}^{\uparrow}(s)=\mathcal{P}\bigl(s,s^{\mathsf{A}^{\ell+1}},(s^{\mathsf{A}^{i}})_{i\in[\ell]}\bigr).

For univariate polynomials we will rely on the following implication of the Chinese remainder theorem.

Lemma 7.2

(​​
[
12
,
37
]
)
.

Let
n^{\prime}
be a non-negative integer and let
P(x)=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n^{\prime}}q_{i}\cdot x^{i}
be a polynomial in one variable
x
of degree at most
n^{\prime}
with integer coefficients satisfying
0\leqslant q_{i}\leqslant 2^{n^{\prime}}
for
i=0,\dots,n^{\prime}
.
Further, let
c
be a non-negative integer.
And let
p_{1},\dots,p_{n^{\prime}}
be prime numbers satisfying
n^{\prime}+1<p_{1}<\dots<p_{n^{\prime}}\leqslant c\cdot(n^{\prime})^{2}
and
\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}p_{i}>2^{n^{\prime}}
and such that for every
i\in[n^{\prime}]
, the value
p_{i}
can be computed in time
(c\cdot n^{\prime})^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
and space
\mathcal{O}(\log n^{\prime}+\log c)
.
Suppose that given an integer
i\in[n^{\prime}]
and a value
s\in\mathbb{F}_{p_{i}}
, the evaluation
P(s)\pmod{p_{i}}
can be computed in time
\mathsf{time}
and space
\mathsf{space}
.
Then the coefficients
q_{1},q_{2},\dots,q_{n^{\prime}}
of
P
can be computed in time
\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{time})\cdot(c\cdot n^{\prime})^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
and space
\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{space}+\log n^{\prime}+\log c)
.

Proof.

This lemma is a slightly adapted version of Theorem 5.3 by Pilipczuk and Wrochna
[
37
]
.
For the sake of completeness we provide the proof of this adapted theorem.
It suffices to describe how to compute a single coefficient
q_{k}
as this can then be repeated for every
k\in[n^{\prime}]_{0}
reusing the same space.
Due to
\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}p_{i}>2^{n^{\prime}}\geqslant q_{k}
, by the Chinese remainder theorem the coefficient
q_{k}
is uniquely determined by the rests
q_{k}\pmod{p_{1}},q_{k}\pmod{p_{2}},\dots,q_{k}\pmod{p_{n^{\prime}}}
.
Moreover, there is an effective version of this theorem
[
12
]
stating that from these rests, the bits of
q_{k}
can be recovered in time
(c\cdot n^{\prime})^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
and space
\mathcal{O}(\log c+\log n^{\prime})
.
For clarification let us remark that this procedure does not store all required rests simultaneously but rather uses the compositionality of logspace algorithms and then outputs the bits of
q_{k}
one after another.

So we remain with the task of computing
q_{k}\pmod{p_{i}}
given some
i\in[n^{\prime}]
.
First, the prime
p_{i}
can be computed in time
(c\cdot n^{\prime})^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
and space
\mathcal{O}(\log n^{\prime}+\log c)
by assumption.
The bit length of
p_{i}
is also upper-bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\log n^{\prime}+\log c)
.
In the same time and space we can compute a generator
\alpha_{i}
of the group
\mathbb{F}^{*}_{p_{i}}
by trying every number
t\in\mathbb{F}_{p_{i}}^{*}
and computing all of its exponents
t^{j}
(with
j\in[p_{i}-1]
).
In their proof Pilipczuk and Wrochna
[
37
]
show that
n^{\prime}<p_{i}-1
implies the equality

q_{k}\equiv_{p_{i}}-\sum_{j=0}^{p_{i}-2}P(\alpha_{i}^{j})\cdot\alpha_{i}^{-jk}.

And this value can now be computed by evaluating
P
at most
(c\cdot n^{\prime})^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
times and carrying out at most
(c\cdot n^{\prime})^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
arithmetic operations in
\mathbb{F}_{p_{i}}
.
Altogether, the rest
q_{k}\pmod{p_{i}}
is computed in time
\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{time})\cdot(c\cdot n^{\prime})^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
and space
\mathcal{O}(\mathsf{space}+\log n^{\prime}+\log c)
so the lemma follows.
∎

By
lemma

5.17
, to check if
G
satisfies
\varphi
, it suffices to iterate over all
\mathtt{F}\subseteq\mathsf{EQ}(\varphi)
, all integers
j\in[2\cdot|\varphi|\cdot d_{\varphi}\cdot n\cdot\mathsf{td}]_{0}
, and all
\bar{c}\in([n\cdot\mathsf{td}]_{0})^{\ell}
and check whether
[\![\varphi]\!]^{(\mathtt{F},\bar{c})}=1
holds and the monomial
\alpha^{j}\beta^{j}\gamma^{\bar{c}}
has a non-zero coefficient in
\mathcal{P}^{=}_{r}(\emptyset,\emptyset,\emptyset,\emptyset,\mathtt{F})
(here
r
denotes the root of the elimination tree
T
).
To carry out this check in a space-efficient way we will make use of
lemma

7.2
.
We now define the set
p_{1},\dots,p_{m}
of primes that will be used in our algorithm.
The choices of
m
and
\mathsf{M}
in the definition below will become clear in the proof of
theorem

7.7
.

Definition 7.3
.

Let
\mathsf{M}=2^{5\cdot n\cdot\mathsf{td}\cdot|\varphi|\cdot\log(d_{\varphi}+1)}
and let
m=(5\cdot n\cdot\mathsf{td}\cdot|\varphi|\cdot d_{\varphi})^{|\ell|+2}
.
Consider the infinite set of the prime numbers having the rest
1
modulo
p_{\varphi}
. We define
p_{1}<p_{2}<\dots<p_{m}
as the
(m+1)
-st,
(m+2)
-nd,
\dots
, and the
(2m)
-th smallest number in this set, respectively.

Lemma 7.4
.

The following properties hold:

1.

m\geqslant\mathsf{A}^{*}
,

2.

2^{m}\geqslant\mathsf{M}
,

3.

\prod_{i\in[m]}p_{i}>2^{m}
,

4.

for
i\in[m]
, we have
